The mpt-crypto library implements cryptographically sound primitives for confidential
transactions on the XRP Ledger. While the core cryptographic operations are correct, the
API design has several areas that could be improved to enhance safety, usability, and
maintainability. This document provides actionable recommendations for hardening the
library interface before deployment.

Missing or Inconsistent Parameter Validation
Most API functions do not validate input pointers before dereferencing them. If a caller
passes NULL, the library will segfault rather than returning an error. Consider the
secp256k1_elgamal_decrypt function, shown in figure D.1, which does not check the
validity of any of its pointer parameters before using them.

    int secp256k1_elgamal_decrypt(

                       Figure D.1: Missing NULL pointer checks in ElGamal encryption

In addition, semantic validation of parameters is performed inconsistently across the
codebase. For example, the prover function
secp256k1_mpt_prove_same_plaintext_multi does not validate the input
randomness array r_array before using it, while the function
secp256k1_elgamal_pedersen_link_prove validates its witness parameter:

    /* 0. Validate Witnesses */
    if (!secp256k1_ec_seckey_verify(ctx, r)) return 0;

                Figure D.2: Witness validation in link proof that is missing in other functions

      PUBLIC​                                          ​                               Cryptographic Review
Recommendation: Add comprehensive NULL pointer checks and parameter validation at
the start of all public API functions to prevent undefined behavior and enable graceful error
handling.

Implicit Length Assumptions
Many functions accept byte array pointers with implicit length assumptions documented
only in comments; an example is shown in figure D.3. This creates a risk of buffer
over-reads if callers provide incorrectly sized buffers.

    int generate_canonical_encrypted_zero(
            const secp256k1_context* ctx,
            secp256k1_pubkey* enc_zero_c1,
            secp256k1_pubkey* enc_zero_c2,
            const secp256k1_pubkey* pubkey,
            const unsigned char* account_id,     // 20 bytes
            const unsigned char* mpt_issuance_id // 24 bytes

                       Figure D.3: Fixed-size byte arrays without explicit length parameters
                                  (mpt-crypto/src/elgamal.c#L211–L217)

Proving and verifying functions similarly accept proof buffers with lengths that are only
documented in the prover implementation rather than validated at the API boundary:

    /**
     * Verifies the proof of equality with shared randomness.
     */
    int secp256k1_mpt_verify_equality_shared_r(
            const secp256k1_context* ctx,
            const unsigned char* proof,
            size_t n,
            const secp256k1_pubkey* C1,
            const secp256k1_pubkey* C2_vec,
            const secp256k1_pubkey* Pk_vec,
            const unsigned char* context_id

                          Figure D.4: Variable-length arrays without bounds validation
                          (mpt-crypto/include/secp256k1_mpt.h#L436–L446)

Recommendation: Add explicit length parameters for all buffer arguments and validate
them at function entry to prevent buffer overflows and over-reads.

Generic Error Codes
Currently, functions return only 0 for error and 1 for success, with no distinction between
different failure modes. This makes debugging difficult, as callers cannot distinguish
between NULL pointer errors, invalid inputs, cryptographic failures, or out-of-range values.

Recommendation: Consider introducing structured error codes (e.g., -1 for NULL pointer,
-2 for invalid input, -3 for crypto failure), as shown in figure D.5.
​
      Trail of Bits​                                    59​        Ripple Labs XRP Ledger Confidential Transfer​
      PUBLIC​                                            ​                               Cryptographic Review
    #define SECP256K1_MPT_OK                    1
    #define SECP256K1_MPT_ERROR                 0
    #define SECP256K1_MPT_ERROR_NULL_POINTER   -1
    #define SECP256K1_MPT_ERROR_INVALID_INPUT -2
    #define SECP256K1_MPT_ERROR_CRYPTO_FAILED -3
    #define SECP256K1_MPT_ERROR_OUT_OF_RANGE   -4
    #define SECP256K1_MPT_ERROR_BUFFER_TOO_SMALL -5

                             Figure D.5: Suggested return code convention

Incomplete Parameter Documentation
The API header file lacks comprehensive documentation for function parameters. Many
functions do not specify whether parameters are input or output, what the required buffer
sizes are, whether pointers can be NULL, or what specific values cause failures.

    int secp256k1_bulletproof_verify_agg(
            const secp256k1_context* ctx,
            const secp256k1_pubkey* G_vec,                   /* length n = 64*m */
            const secp256k1_pubkey* H_vec,                   /* length n = 64*m */
            const unsigned char* proof,
            size_t proof_len,
            const secp256k1_pubkey* commitment_C_vec,        /* length m */
            size_t m,
            const secp256k1_pubkey* pk_base,
            const unsigned char* context_id

                              Figure D.6: Missing function documentation
                       (mpt-crypto/include/secp256k1_mpt.h#L459–L468)

Recommendation: Adopt comprehensive Doxygen-style documentation specifying
input/output semantics, required buffer sizes, NULL-ability, thread safety, side-channel
properties, and specific failure conditions for all public API functions.

Adoption of a Unified Coding Standard
The codebase lacks consistent coding conventions for control flow and error handling.
Some functions use multiple return statements throughout the code, while others use
single-exit patterns with goto cleanup. Some functions validate inputs incrementally,
while others batch validation at the start. An example is shown in figure D.7.

    free(L_vec);
    free(R_vec);

    return ok ? 1 : 0;

    fail:
    free(L_vec);
    free(R_vec);
    return 0;


​
      Trail of Bits​                             60​        Ripple Labs XRP Ledger Confidential Transfer​
      PUBLIC​                                     ​                               Cryptographic Review
                     Figure D.7: Inconsistent error handling and control flow patterns
                   (mpt-crypto/src/bulletproof_aggregated.c#L2178–L2186)

Recommendation: Establish and document unified coding standards covering parameter
validation order, error handling patterns (single-exit versus multiple returns), memory
allocation strategy, and naming conventions to improve code consistency and
maintainability.

Lack of Unified Data Structures
Proofs are stored as separate arrays of values that are passed individually to proof
generation and verification functions (see figure D.8, for example). This fragmented
approach reduces API ergonomics and creates opportunities for integration errors when
callers must manually coordinate multiple related parameters.

    SECP256K1_API int secp256k1_mpt_prove_same_plaintext_multi(
            const secp256k1_context* ctx,
            unsigned char* proof_out,
            size_t* proof_len,
            uint64_t amount_m,
            size_t n_ciphertexts,
            const secp256k1_pubkey* R_array,
            const secp256k1_pubkey* S_array,
            const secp256k1_pubkey* Pk_array,
            const unsigned char* r_array, // Flat array: r1 || r2 || ... (N * 32 bytes)
            const unsigned char* tx_context_id
    );

                        Figure D.8: Related proof values passed as separate arrays
                       (mpt-crypto/include/secp256k1_mpt.h#L239–L250)

In the example, the caller must manually ensure that R_array, S_array, Pk_array, and
r_array all have exactly n_ciphertexts elements. If these arrays have mismatched
lengths, the function will read out of bounds or use incorrect data, but the API provides no
mechanism to detect this error at compile time or runtime.

Similarly, proof outputs are returned as raw byte buffers rather than structured types,
requiring callers to understand the internal binary layout.

Recommendation: Introduce structured types that collect related values (e.g.,
secp256k1_mpt_ciphertext grouping R and S components, secp256k1_mpt_proof
wrapping proof buffers) to improve readability, enable compile-time validation, and provide
a more ergonomic API to library users.

Non-Distinguished Data Types
Throughout the mpt-crypto codebase, the secp256k1_pubkey type is consistently used
to represent elliptic curve points used more generally in ElGamal encryption, Pedersen

​
      Trail of Bits​                               61​       Ripple Labs XRP Ledger Confidential Transfer​
      PUBLIC​                                       ​                              Cryptographic Review
commitments, Bulletproofs, and other contexts. However, it is also used in other portions
of the protocol codebase to represent actual public keys. This can lead to confusion and
variable misuse, potentially putting sensitive values at risk.

Recommendation: Create different types to separate distinct uses of the same data
structures. From a performance and compile-time perspective, typedef is free. However,
when coupled with suitable compiler flags (such as -Wpedantic), it can identify when
cross-uses of the same data structure occur, catching potential errors. Since valid
cross-uses can be resolved by casting, false positives can be easily resolved.

    PUBLIC​                                  ​                              Cryptographic Review

#88 [open] *_verify_batch: batched verification for compact sigma + aggregated BP https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/88
Add `*_verify_batch` entry points for the compact AND-composed
sigma family (`proof_compact_standard`, `proof_compact_convertback`,
`proof_compact_clawback`, `proof_pok_sk`) and the aggregated
Bulletproof. Consumes `mpt_msm_variable_time` (landed via the
`ecmult_multi_var` vendor work).

## Mechanisms

- **BP**: random-linear-combination batching via BBB+18 §6.1
  generator stacking. Weights $\rho_i = \rho^{i-1}$ from
  $\rho = H(\text{tag} \,\|\, \pi_1 \,\|\, \cdots \,\|\, \pi_n)$.
  Soundness $(n-1)/q$ via Schwartz–Zippel. Mixed-`m` batches
  are fine: BBB+18 stacking shares generator vectors `G, H`, and
  proofs with different `m` just touch different prefix lengths
  of those vectors.
- **Compact sigma**: cross-proof MSM amortisation on the
  reconstruction step (shared CMPT generators); not RLC-style.
  Per-proof soundness inherits unchanged. Hash equality on
  reconstructed $T_{i,j}$ is per-proof.

Realistic gain at $n = 64$: 2–4× validator throughput on the
cryptographic path on top of the single-proof MSM speedup from #100.

## Prerequisites

- `mpt_msm_variable_time` landed (in flight, #89).
- #100 (apply vendored MSM to single-BP verification). Builds the
  batching layer on top of an already-MSM-accelerated single-proof
  verifier rather than directly on the public-API loop. Lower
  risk, cleaner review surface, and the single-BP speedup is
  independently useful for transactions with one BP proof.

## Out of scope for this issue

- rippled-side integration (tracked separately in rippled).
- `mpt_msm_constant_time` profile (#87 — not on the verifier path).
---
#106 [open] perf(sigma): VT scalar mul for sigma reconstruction — measurement / discussion (#88 sigma side) https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/pull/106
**Draft / measurement PR.** The empirical win is small (~1.5–2% per
verifier). Posted for visibility and to document why the literal #88
ask ("cross-proof MSM amortisation on the reconstruction step")
isn't achievable with the current MSM surface. Stacked on **#89**
(vendor MSM); orthogonal to #104 / #105.

See #88 for the parallel discussion comment.

## TL;DR

| Verifier | Baseline (CT) | After VT-mul | Delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| compact_standard (n=3) | 0.220 ms | 0.215 ms | **−2.3%** |
| compact_clawback | 0.064 ms | 0.063 ms | **−1.6%** |
| compact_convertback | 0.086 ms | 0.085 ms | **−1.2%** |
| pok_sk | 0.021 ms | 0.020 ms | **−2.0%** |

(Apple M-series, 5000 iters/verifier per run, 3 runs averaged. All 12
ctests green. Pre-commit clean.)

## What this PR does

- Adds `mpt_ec_pubkey_mul_var` to the vendored libsecp256k1 TU: a thin
  variable-time, in-place scalar mul on an arbitrary point. Routes
  through `secp256k1_ecmult` (the VT engine that powers
  `ecmult_multi_var`) with **no scratch space**, avoiding the per-call
  alloc that dominates at small k.
- Crucially, pubkey ↔ ge conversion uses the **internal 64-byte storage
  layout** (matching libsecp256k1's own `pubkey_load`/`pubkey_save`),
  not `secp256k1_ec_pubkey_parse`. The SEC1-compressed parse does a
  sqrt on every call — going through that swamps the VT savings.
- The four compact-sigma verifiers (`compact_standard`,
  `compact_clawback`, `compact_convertback`, `pok_sk`) swap their
  **reconstruction-side** `secp256k1_ec_pubkey_tweak_mul` calls (CT,
  `ecmult_const`) for `mpt_ec_pubkey_mul_var` (VT).
- **Prover-side `tweak_mul` calls (alpha, delta, gamma, t_sk, t_rho —
  all secret nonces) are left as CT exactly as before.** The CT
  contract is preserved at every prover-side call site.
- New `tests/bench_compact_sigma` microbench (not registered with
  ctest; `cmake --build build --target bench_compact_sigma`).

## Why so little? — empirical analysis

Issue #88 asks for "cross-proof MSM amortisation on the reconstruction
step (shared CMPT generators)". Working through what that can actually
mean for compact-sigma:

1. **Compact-sigma's per-proof check is hash-bound**: 
   `SHA256(reconstructed T_1, T_{2,i}, …) == e`, not algebraic. So
   unlike the BP-side batching landed in #105, you **cannot
   random-linear-combine across proofs and check identity** — SHA-256
   has no linear-algebra-friendly batching identity.

2. The only mechanism that *can* amortise across proofs sharing
   generators G/H is a **multi-output MSM with shared Pippenger
   windowing**. libsecp256k1's `secp256k1_ecmult_multi_var` is
   single-output. Adding a multi-output variant would be ~500 LOC of
   new vendored code plus a substantial audit; that's its own issue
   if we want to pursue it.

3. At per-equation granularity (k = 1..3, which compact-sigma
   reconstructions are), calling `mpt_msm_variable_time` once per
   equation is a **net regression** because the scratch alloc
   dominates the VT-vs-CT savings (measured −10% on pok_sk in
   exploratory commits).

4. So the only available knob is "drop in a VT scalar mul" for the
   per-equation `tweak_mul` calls. Even then, on libsecp256k1 0.7.1
   the CT engine `ecmult_const` is heavily optimized (GLV endomorphism
   + scalar split + tightly tuned assembly), so the VT savings are
   small.

## What this is *not*

- **Cross-proof MSM amortisation.** Not achievable here for the
  reasons above.
- **Multi-output Pippenger.** The only path to a real 2–4× sigma-side
  win, but disproportionate effort vs. estimated gain. Needs its own
  scoping issue.
- **A change to the BP-side work.** Independent from #104 / #105.

## Should this merge?

Judgment call. Arguments **for**:
- ~2% is real and free for users.
- The new VT primitive is an **audit-visible marker**
  (`tweak_mul` ⇒ CT, `mul_var` ⇒ VT) that documents verifier-side
  timing safety at the call site.

Arguments **against**:
- 2% may not survive across hardware / compiler / libsecp updates.
- Adds public API surface that must be maintained.
- Doesn't deliver what #88's title literally asks for.

I lean toward not merging in its current form, but the data is worth
having on the record. **Draft** for that reason.

## Out of scope (tracked separately)

- Multi-output Pippenger for true cross-proof MSM amortisation —
  needs its own issue.
- `mpt_msm_constant_time` for the prover path — **#87**.
- rippled-side integration.

#89 [open] feat(msm): vendor libsecp256k1 ecmult_multi_var as mpt_msm_variable_time https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/pull/89
Self-contained vendor of `secp256k1_ecmult_multi_var` (Pippenger/Straus
MSM) from libsecp256k1 v0.7.1, supporting the upcoming `*_verify_batch`
API for compact-sigma + aggregated-BP batch verification (#88).
Companion CT prover-side profile tracked in #87.

## Vendor layout (`third_party/secp256k1-msm/`)

- 39 `.h` files + `precomputed_ecmult.c` (~2.3 MB of generator table
  data) copied from libsecp256k1 `src/` at tag `v0.7.1`, commit
  `1a53f4961f337b4d166c25fce72ef0dc88806618`. See `PROVENANCE`.
- `mpt_msm.c`: single wrapper translation unit that includes the
  vendored headers and exposes the one external API symbol,
  `mpt_msm_variable_time`. All upstream functions are file-static;
  no link-time collision with the still-linked libsecp256k1.
- Two compile-time `-D` renames (`-Dsecp256k1_pre_g=mpt_secp256k1_pre_g`
  and the `_128` variant) to namespace the only non-static data
  symbols (the generator precomputation tables, which the linked
  libsecp256k1 also exports).
- Local edit to `util.h`: relative include `"../include/secp256k1.h"`
  changed to `<secp256k1.h>` so it resolves through the linked
  install. Only edit to upstream sources.

## Self-containment

The vendor includes dependent internal types (`secp256k1_gej`,
`secp256k1_scalar`, `secp256k1_fe`, scratch-space layouts), not just
`ecmult_impl.h` and the WNAF helpers. This decouples the vendored
MSM's correctness from the linked libsecp256k1 binary's version, so
**this PR does not change the existing libsecp256k1 Conan pin**.

## Threat model

`mpt_msm_variable_time` is variable-time. Validator path only; the
verifier has no secret inputs. The two-profile API shape
(`mpt_msm_variable_time` vs the planned `mpt_msm_constant_time` —
#87) makes the constant-time requirement audit-visible at the call
site. Full threat-model treatment in `cmpt-ct-and-batch.tex`
(`internal-ripplex-research`, `proposed-changes-writeup` branch).

## Build integration

- CMake `OBJECT` library `mpt-crypto-msm-vendor`; linked into
  `mpt-crypto`.
- `-Wno-pedantic` only on the vendored TU; main library remains
  under `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`.
- `.pre-commit-config.yaml`: `third_party/` excluded from style
  hooks so the vendored upstream files stay close to upstream form
  (clean diffs on re-sync). Large-file limit raised to 4 MB to
  admit `precomputed_ecmult.c`.

## Tests

`tests/test_mpt_msm.c`: calibration test that compares the
vendored MSM output to a reference computed via the public
libsecp256k1 API (`tweak_mul` + `pubkey_combine` in a loop) on
randomized inputs. Bit-identical match required across
`N_TRIALS`. Confirms scalar/point encoding conventions,
group-element layout, and end-to-end correctness of the vendoring.

All 11 existing tests continue to pass.

## Drift detection

None. We follow the same operational model as the Conan lockfile
under `conan/lockfile/` (#95): pin known-good state, re-sync
deliberately when there is a reason (security fix, performance
gain). No automated tripwire — same as for Conan-managed
dependencies.

## Out of scope (tracked separately)

- `mpt_msm_constant_time` profile for the prover path: **#87**.
- `*_verify_batch` API for the four sigma proofs and aggregated
  BP: **#88**.
- rippled-side integration.
- Any change to the existing libsecp256k1 dep pin.

#101 [open] [Enhancement] Extend ElGamal decryption range beyond 10^6 (BSGS or Pollard's kangaroo) https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/101
## Summary

`secp256k1_elgamal_decrypt` brute-forces $i \in [1, 10^6]$ only.  The
Bulletproof range proof attests $v \in [0, 2^{64})$ and the protocol's
`maxMPTokenAmount` is $2^{63} - 1$, so the decryptable range is roughly
$10^{13}\times$ smaller than the proof / protocol range.

For any issuance with `sfAssetScale >= 6` (e.g. a USD-pegged stablecoin
where 1 USD = $10^6$ atomic units), normal balances exceed the
decryptable range.  Cumulative homomorphic addition can also push a
spending balance past $10^6$ without any single transaction crossing
it.  Once past the cap, the holder cannot construct ConvertBack /
Send (both need the plaintext balance for proof generation), and the
issuer cannot clawback locked amounts.  The funds are recoverable only
via off-chain BSGS.

The $10^6$ cap is the deliberate constant-iteration budget restored
by #99 (commit `316197ac`) addressing TOB-RIPCTXR-1.  It is _not_ a
bug to fix in-place; the right move is a larger-range solver.

## Scope

1. Add a baby-step giant-step (BSGS) or Pollard's kangaroo solver
   to `mpt-crypto` (likely `src/elgamal.c` or a new
   `src/dlp_solver.c`).
2. Target a default decryptable range of $\sim 2^{32}$ (with a few-MB
   precomputed table for BSGS) or $\sim 2^{40}$ (kangaroo, no table,
   higher expected-time).
3. Expose the range as a compile-time / runtime parameter so the
   on-chain verification path (which does not decrypt) and the
   off-chain wallet path can use different budgets.
4. Update the public-header doxygen and `cmpt-compact-sigma` /
   XLS-0096 documentation with the new range.
5. Regression tests at the boundary and well past $10^6$.

## Acceptance criteria

- Decryption succeeds for amounts up to the configured upper bound
  in bounded time / memory.
- The constant-iteration property is preserved within each
  configured range (TOB-RIPCTX-4 timing-leak fix is not regressed).
- Issuer clawback path works for amounts $> 10^6$.

## References

- Sherlock #1566 (High): _ElGamal decrypt hard-capped at 1,000,000_.
- Sherlock #2308 (Medium): _Token holders will lose the ability to
  decrypt their confidential balance_.
- Sherlock #2196 (Invalid; same root cause).
- TOB-RIPCTX-1 long-term recommendation (already deferred from the
  fix-review scope).
- Project: `paper/cmpt-progress-and-changes.tex`,
  `\paragraph{ElGamal decrypt $10^6$ cap vs $2^{64}$ proof range}`
  in `\section{Sherlock Audit (April--May 2026) Cross-Reference}`.
---
#105 [open] perf(bp): batched verify_batch_agg via BBB+18 RLC stacking (part of #88) https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/pull/105
Part of #88 (BP-side only; sigma-side amortisation will land as a
separate PR). Stacked on **#104** (single-proof MSM, #100) and **#89**
(vendored MSM). Only the last commit (`01f118ac`) is the #88 change.
Review just that commit; the earlier two are already up for review on
their own PRs.

## What

New public API:

```c
int secp256k1_bulletproof_verify_batch_agg(
    secp256k1_context const* ctx,
    secp256k1_pubkey const* G_vec,  /* sized for max(m_vec[i]) */
    secp256k1_pubkey const* H_vec,
    unsigned char const* const* proofs,
    size_t const* proof_lens,
    secp256k1_pubkey const* const* commitment_C_vecs,
    size_t const* m_vec,
    secp256k1_pubkey const* pk_base,
    unsigned char const* const* context_ids,
    size_t n_proofs);
```

Verifies `n_proofs` aggregated Bulletproofs in a single
`mpt_msm_variable_time` call via BBB+18 §6.1 random-linear-combination
stacking. Mixed-m batches are fine: `G_vec / H_vec` are sized for the
largest m and lower-m proofs touch only a prefix.

## Equation

For each proof $i$, the same $E_{1,i} + c_i \cdot E_{2,i} = 0$ residual
from #104 holds. The batch verifier checks

$$
\sum_i \rho^i \cdot (E_{1,i} + c_i \cdot E_{2,i}) = 0
$$

with $\rho = H(\texttt{\"MPT\_BP\_VERIFY\_BATCH\"} \,\|\, c_0 \,\|\, \cdots \,\|\, c_{n-1})$.
Each $c_i$ transitively binds proof $i$'s bytes (incl. commitments and
context_id) via the FS chain, so $\rho$ binds every batch input.

The shared generators $G_\text{vec}$, $H_\text{vec}$, $H_\text{pk}$, $U$
contribute **one MSM term each** regardless of batch size because their
per-proof coefficients are summed into accumulators before the MSM is
built. This collapses $\sim B \cdot (2n + 2 \log n + m + 6)$ terms down
to $(2 \cdot n_\text{max} + 2) + B \cdot (m + 4 + 2 \log n)$ — i.e., the
dominant $2n$ work is paid once for the whole batch.

## Soundness

A single invalid proof in the batch makes its residual non-zero, and
Schwartz–Zippel on the freshly-derived $\rho$ gives $\le n_\text{proofs}/q$
rejection probability for a malicious batch. $\rho = 0$ is rejected
explicitly (negligibly probable).

## Refactor

The per-proof derivation (parse, FS $y/z/x$, $\delta$, $y^{-k}$ powers,
IPA round challenges $u_i / u_i^{-1}$, $s_k$ vector, `ipa_transcript_id`,
$u_x$, intra-proof $c$) is now factored into `bp_proof_state_init` /
`bp_proof_state_free`. `secp256k1_bulletproof_verify_agg` from #104 now
uses the same state setup as the batch verifier; single-proof verify is
just a batch of one with $\rho = 1$.

## Perf (Apple M-series, m=2)

| Batch size | serial verify | batched verify | speedup |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 8  | 13.3 ms (1.66 ms/proof) |  3.46 ms (0.43 ms/proof) | **3.85×** |
| 64 | 108 ms  (1.69 ms/proof) | 18.0 ms (0.28 ms/proof) | **5.98×** |

Beats #88's 2–4× estimate; the extra factor is from the
shared-generator amortisation. At $B = 64$, the 256 $G_k + H_k$ slots
are paid once instead of 64 times.

## Tests

New `tests/test_bulletproof_batch.c` covers:

- $n_\text{proofs} \in \{1, 2, 8, 64\}$ with uniform $m = 2$
- $n_\text{proofs} = 4$ with mixed $m \in \{1, 2\}$
- positive: each proof verifies individually AND batch verifies
- negative: tamper the last proof's last commitment → batch must
  reject (exercises the shared-accumulator rejection path)
- throughput benchmark vs serial (B=8 and B=64)

All 12 ctests green. Pre-commit + clang-format clean.

## Out of scope (tracked separately)

- Cross-proof MSM amortisation for the four compact sigma families
  (also mentioned in #88) — separate mechanism (no RLC; just shared
  CMPT generators); ship as a follow-up PR.
- rippled-side integration.
- `mpt_msm_constant_time` for the prover path — **#87**.
---
#112 [open] [API Hardening] Decide and document zero / point-at-infinity handling at every API boundary https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/112
**Severity:** Informational; clarity / future-defense.
**Source:** TOB May 2026 revision, Appendix D — "Malformed scalar
and point edge cases" (pp. 63-64).

## Problem

`libsecp256k1`'s `seckey_*` helpers reject zero by design (because
zero is not a valid ECDSA secret key), but the CMPT protocol has
mathematical scalars that can legitimately be zero (amounts,
balances, response scalars in negligible-probability cases) and
mathematical points that can legitimately be the identity
(homomorphic differences, MSM intermediates). Mixing the two
conventions has caused several historical bugs in this codebase
(#37/#38, #39, #41, #51).

Each API boundary should make an **explicit, documented choice**
between three options:

- **Reject as malformed input** — return an error.
- **Treat as a sentinel** — encode a protocol-specific
  out-of-band representation (e.g., the 33-zero-bytes BP
  transcript convention from #59).
- **Accept as a valid result** — produce identity / zero output.

## Recommended change

For every API that can be reached with `zero` scalar input or
`identity` point output, document the decision in the doxygen, add
tests that cover both the "zero is valid" and "zero is rejected"
cases, and ensure internal helpers don't silently fail when
encountering it.

TOB-specific call sites to cover (per Appendix D p. 63-64):

- `compute_sigma_response` (mpt_internal.h:L116-L117) — routes
  zero witnesses through `scalar_*` helpers, not `seckey_*`. **Done**
  via #37/#38 (PR #58).
- `compute_amount_point` — documents that amount=0 cannot be
  encoded as a non-identity pubkey. **Done** via #39 (PR #59).
- The four compact-proof verifiers reject zero proof scalars via
  `secp256k1_ec_seckey_verify`: `secp256k1_mpt_pok_sk_verify`,
  `secp256k1_compact_standard_verify`,
  `secp256k1_compact_convertback_verify`,
  `secp256k1_compact_clawback_verify`. Document the choice
  explicitly in each doxygen (current code does it implicitly).
- Secret scalar inputs are rejected when zero in
  `secp256k1_mpt_pedersen_commit`,
  `secp256k1_bulletproof_create_commitment`, and the compact
  convertback prover. Decide whether zero is invalid input or a
  valid mathematical edge case for each API.
- Homomorphic / commitment-difference paths can produce
  point-at-infinity: `secp256k1_elgamal_add` (RIPCTXR-5, tracked
  in #102), `secp256k1_elgamal_subtract`,
  `mpt_compute_convert_back_remainder`,
  `mpt_verify_send_range_proof`. Each path should document whether
  infinity is a malformed input, an expected zero result, or a
  separate sentinel case.

## Acceptance

- Every API boundary listed above has a doxygen sentence stating
  the zero / identity handling decision.
- Each has at least one test case exercising the zero / identity
  input or output explicitly.
- The current code's behavior is preserved; this is a
  documentation + test-coverage issue, not a behavior change
  (except for any specific bugs found during the sweep).

## References

- TOB May 2026 Appendix D, pp. 63-64.
- Already-closed antecedents: #37, #38, #39, #41, #51, #59.
- Related: #102 (RIPCTXR-5 point-at-infinity griefing).
---
#102 [closed] [Bug] secp256k1_elgamal_add fails on identity point — enables MergeInbox griefing https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/102
## Summary

`secp256k1_elgamal_add` in `src/elgamal.c` calls
`secp256k1_ec_pubkey_combine` for both ciphertext components (lines
202, 207).  Per libsecp256k1's API, that function returns 0 when the
result is the group identity (the point at infinity, which has no
compressed serialization).  The library propagates the failure as
`nullopt` to the rippled caller, and
`ConfidentialMPTMergeInbox::doApply` exits with `tecINTERNAL`
without applying any state change.

This is reachable from public, well-formed transactions:

1. Right after a holder's first `ConfidentialMPTConvert`, the
   spending-balance `c1 = r_c · G` where
   $r_c = \mathrm{SHA256}(\text{``EncZero''} \| \text{accountID} \|
   \text{mptIssuanceID})$ is publicly derivable from on-ledger data
   (anyone can re-run `generate_canonical_encrypted_zero`).
2. The Convert blinding factor `r_v0` is stored as
   `sfBlindingFactor` on the Convert transaction, also publicly
   visible.
3. An attacker submits a `ConfidentialMPTSend` to the victim with
   shared blinding factor $r_{\mathrm{atk}} = -(r_c + r_{v0}) \bmod q$.
4. The victim's next `ConfidentialMPTMergeInbox` sums
   $r_c \cdot G + (r_{v0} + r_{\mathrm{atk}}) \cdot G = O$, the
   point-at-infinity branch of `pubkey_combine` triggers, and the
   merge fails deterministically.  Every retry fails identically;
   the victim cannot Send / ConvertBack either (zero spending balance
   breaks `compute_sigma_response`).

Sherlock #2759 documents the attack with an end-to-end PoC against
rippled.  This is also TOB-RIPCTXR-5.

## Scope

Library-side mitigation (the focus of this issue): make
`secp256k1_elgamal_add` (and `secp256k1_elgamal_sub` for symmetry)
treat the identity point as a valid canonical-zero ciphertext.
Concretely, replace the bare `pubkey_combine` call with a helper
that:

1. Calls `secp256k1_ec_pubkey_combine`.
2. On failure, checks whether the inputs are additive inverses (via
   per-coordinate equality after one negation) and, if so, returns
   the canonical encoded zero point.
3. Returns `0` only on genuine input-validity errors.

The encoded zero needs a fixed sentinel representation in the
`(c1, c2)` byte layout so downstream comparators (proof inputs,
SLE serialization) handle it uniformly.  Either:
- a 33-byte all-zero `c1` / `c2` sentinel (consistent with the
  existing `33-zero-byte transcript sentinel` for $m \cdot G$ used
  by PR #59), or
- a fixed canonical generator point reserved for the zero state.

The choice has wire-format implications and should be co-designed
with the rippled team and the spec.

## Out of scope

- Rippled-side preflight check (separate item; tracked under
  TOB-RIPCTXR-5 long-term recommendation).
- Making `generate_canonical_encrypted_zero` unpredictable
  (separate design discussion — the determinism is intentional
  to avoid SLE bloat).

## Acceptance criteria

- `secp256k1_elgamal_add` succeeds when the result is the identity,
  returning a canonical encoded-zero ciphertext.
- Regression test exercising the
  $r_c + r_{v0} + r_{\mathrm{atk}} \equiv 0$ scenario from
  Sherlock #2759.
- Documented in `cmpt-compact-sigma` and XLS-0096.

## References

- Sherlock #2759 (Medium): _Inbox poisoning via crafted Send
  blinding factor drives MergeInbox into the point-at-infinity
  branch of homomorphicAdd_.
- Sherlock #986 (Invalid; same root cause).
- TOB-RIPCTXR-5 (High; covered in
  `paper/cmpt-progress-and-changes.tex`,
  `\subsubsection*{RIPCTXR-5}` and
  `\section{MergeInbox Redesign}`).
---
#18 [merged] feat: compact AND-composed sigma proof for standard EC-ElGamal (192 bytes) https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/pull/18
## Summary

Adds a compact, AND-composed sigma proof (`SECP256K1_COMPACT_STANDARD_PROOF_SIZE = 192 bytes`) that ties together the full set of constraints for a confidential multi-recipient transfer under standard EC-ElGamal:

| Constraint | Proves |
|---|---|
| `C1 = r·G` | Correct ephemeral key |
| `C2_i = r·pk_i + m·G` for all i | Correct encryption to each recipient |
| `PC_m = r·G + m·H` | Amount commitment binds to encrypted amount |
| `pk_A = sk_A·G` | Prover knows sender secret key |
| `PC_b = r_b·G + v·H` | Balance commitment well-formed |
| `sk_A·C1_rem + v·G = C2_rem` | Balance/remainder check |

All six relations are proven in a single Fiat-Shamir compressed proof `(e, z_r, z_m, z_sk, z_rb, z_v)` under the domain tag `"CMPT_COMBINED_STD_PROOF"`.

A full discussion, motivation, and formal specification of this proof system — including protocol description, security analysis, and the compact-form Fiat-Shamir transformation — is in [`paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf`](paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf) (source: [`paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex`](paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex)).

**No changes to existing public API.** The two new functions (`secp256k1_compact_standard_prove` / `secp256k1_compact_standard_verify`) and the size macro are purely additive.

### New files

- `src/proof_compact_standard.c` — prover + verifier
- `tests/test_compact_standard.c` — 5 test cases (positive, wrong context, tampered C1/C2, corrupted proof byte, wrong PC_b)
- `paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` — full writeup and specification
- `paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex` — LaTeX source

### Modified files

- `include/secp256k1_mpt.h` — new API section at end of file
- `CMakeLists.txt` — adds new source file
- `tests/CMakeLists.txt` — adds new test target

This PR is stacked on top of #17 (cleanup-internal-helpers); the diff against `main` includes that refactor. The meaningful diff is the single commit on top of the cleanup branch.

## Test plan

- [ ] `cmake --build . && ctest` — all tests pass including `test_compact_standard`
- [ ] Positive prove/verify roundtrip succeeds
- [ ] Wrong `context_id` → verify returns 0
- [ ] Tampered `C1` or `C2_vec` → verify returns 0
- [ ] Single flipped proof byte → verify returns 0
- [ ] Wrong `PC_b` → verify returns 0

#98 [merged] docs(paper): bind ΔP_i into Fiat--Shamir challenge in shared-r Schnorr https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/pull/98
Strengthens the Fiat–Shamir transcript of the shared-r Schnorr proof
in `paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex` by including the public bases
$\Delta P_2, \dots, \Delta P_n$ in the challenge hash, alongside the
existing inputs $\Delta D_2, \dots, \Delta D_n$ and the announcements
$R_G, R_2, \dots, R_n$.

## Why

Omitting $\Delta P_i$ lets a malicious prover, after seeing the
challenge $c$, choose
$$\Delta P_i = s^{-1}(R_i + c \cdot \Delta D_i)$$
for arbitrary $R_i$ so that the verifier's reconstruction equation
holds for a statement different from the one the prover committed
to. Including $\Delta P_i$ in the hash binds the challenge to the
full public statement and closes that hole.

## Diff scope

Just `paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.{tex,pdf}`. Single TeX edit:

- Challenge derivation now reads
  $c = H(C \,\|\, \Delta D_2 \,\|\, \cdots \,\|\, \Delta D_n
        \,\|\, \Delta P_2 \,\|\, \cdots \,\|\, \Delta P_n
        \,\|\, R_G \,\|\, R_2 \,\|\, \cdots \,\|\, R_n)$.
- Verifier reconstruction equation updated to match.
- Defensive paragraph added immediately below the challenge
  definition explaining the malleability attack the inclusion
  prevents.

Proof size unchanged (64 bytes, independent of $n$).

## Implementation

This PR is paper-only. No code change required — the implementations
in `proof_compact_*.c` and `proof_pok_sk.c` already include all the
public statement points in their respective Fiat–Shamir transcripts;
the paper was lagging.

Mirrors the update in the private `confidential-transactions` working
copy at commit `a96c137`. (Internal cross-ref:
`internal-ripplex-research:compact-sigma-writeup` branch was synced
ahead of this PR.)

#107 [merged] fix(crypto): self-describing IKM for generate_deterministic_nonces (closes #92, RIPCTXR-10) https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/pull/107
Closes #92. Addresses **TOB-RIPCTXR-10** (Severity Informational, May
2026 revision audit).


## ~~⚠️ Wire-format-breaking — needs rippled-team coordination before merge~~
**EDIT 6/9/26:** this concern is overstated here, since the HKDF is (currently) only used
internally in `mpt-crypto` to derive nonces for the prover, which is already non-deterministic.

This PR changes the HKDF-Extract IKM layout that derives the
deterministic nonces underpinning every compact-sigma proof. **Every
transcript derived through `generate_deterministic_nonces` rederives
with new nonce material.** Concretely, that's every:

- `ConfidentialMPTSend` proof (`proof_compact_standard.c`)
- `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack` proof (`proof_compact_convertback.c`)
- `Clawback` proof (`proof_compact_clawback.c`)
- `PoK of secret key` proof (`proof_pok_sk.c`)

The aggregated Bulletproof verifier is **not** affected — BP uses its
own nonce derivation, not this helper.

**Please hold merge until the rippled team confirms no on-chain
artifacts (snapshotted transcripts, cross-implementation parity
vectors, mainnet pre-deployment expectations) depend on the previous
layout.** The TOB report classifies this as Informational, so we're
not on a clock — taking the time to sync is appropriate. Pinging
@mrtcnk for review and routing to the rippled integration owner for
sign-off on the wire-format break.

## What changes

**Before:**
```
IKM = witness || statement_hash || domain
```

**After:**
```
IKM = version_tag (4) || k (4 BE) || witness_len (4 BE) ||
      domain_len (4 BE) || witness || statement_hash || domain
```

- `version_tag` = ASCII `"MPT1"` (reserves a future-proof switch-over
  point if the layout ever needs to evolve further)
- `k`, `witness_len`, `domain_len` = `uint32` big-endian

Total: 16-byte length-prefix preamble. Per the issue body's exact
encoding spec.

## Why

The TOB report calls out the prior layout as a **brittle-API
concern**, not a present-day exploit:

> "Current call sites use distinct domains and fixed-width witnesses,
> so no concrete collision exists. However, the helper does not
> encode `witness_len`, `domain_len`, the nonce count `k`, or a
> layout/version identifier into the IKM. Two future proof modules
> with different witness packings could derive the same IKM from
> semantically distinct inputs and reuse the same nonce material
> across distinct sigma-protocol challenges, leaking witnesses via
> the public responses."

The length-prefix preamble makes the IKM unambiguous: no future
witness/domain packing can construct the same IKM from semantically
distinct inputs, even if the concatenated tails happen to coincide.

## Soundness

Purely a domain-separation strengthening on the PRK derivation:

- HKDF-Extract still uses HMAC-SHA256.
- Salt unchanged (32 bytes `RAND_bytes`, defense-in-depth).
- Expand step unchanged (counter-based HMAC-SHA256, sub_counter
  retry loop for the negligible `output == 0 mod n` case).
- PRK distribution remains uniform under the HMAC PRF assumption.

No change to call-site signatures (the helper already took `k`,
`witness_len`, `domain_len` as parameters; only the internal IKM
construction changes).

## Tests

10/10 ctest green. The four compact-sigma test suites
(`test_compact_standard`, `test_compact_convertback`,
`test_compact_clawback`, `test_pok_sk`) exercise prove-then-verify
roundtrips end-to-end with the new IKM. There are no stored
cross-implementation test vectors in this repo, so vector
regeneration in the issue body's acceptance criteria is a downstream
concern for any integration suite that snapshots transcripts (also
why rippled-team coordination matters before merge).

Pre-commit + clang-format clean.

## Out of scope

- rippled-side integration suite refresh (separate repo).
- Long-term migration to a versioned IKM family — `"MPT1"` reserves
  the switch-over slot if/when that's needed.

## References

- TOB May 2026 revision report, Finding 10 (TOB-RIPCTXR-10).
- Progress tracker (`paper/cmpt-progress-and-changes.tex` in
  `confidential-transactions` / `internal-ripplex-research`):
  RIPCTXR-10 row, was "No open PR", now this PR.

  1575	    In commitment form, twisted ElGamal saves bytes primarily by
  1576	    eliminating linkage proofs whose cost comes from group-element
  1577	    commitments ($33$~B each).
  1578	    Compact form removes \emph{all} group-element commitments from
  1579	    \emph{both} schemes.
  1580	    What remains is the irreducible per-witness cost: each independent
  1581	    scalar witness contributes one $32$-byte response.
  1582	    Twisted ElGamal has three witnesses $(r, m, \sk_A)$ whereas
  1583	    standard EC-ElGamal has five $(m, r, b, \rho, \sk_A)$, because
  1584	    the dual-purpose ciphertext/commitment structure of twisted
  1585	    ElGamal makes separate Pedersen commitments---and their associated
  1586	    witnesses $\rho$ and~$b$---unnecessary.
  1587	\end{enumerate}
  1588	
  1589	\noindent
  1590	The $64$-byte gap is \textbf{structural and irreducible}: it comes
  1591	from the number of independent scalar witnesses, not from group
  1592	elements.
  1593	No further compression technique---including the $\mathcal{O}(1)$
  1594	equality proof of \cref{sec:equality-o1}---can close it.
  1595	Adding or removing auditors changes the ciphertext count by
  1596	$\pm 33$~bytes per recipient, identically for both schemes, so the
  1597	relative comparison is unaffected by~$n$.
  1598

  1056	
  1057	\noindent
  1058	Note that the $n = 4$ baseline (A) coincidentally also equals
  1059	$1604$~bytes: Cenk's shared-$C_1$ formulation with $4$ recipients
  1060	happens to match Dore's per-CT formulation with $3$ recipients
  1061	(the extra $C_{2,4}$ adds $33$~B while deduplicating $C_1$ saves
  1062	$33$~B, and the separate same-plaintext proof adds $33$~B while
  1063	losing one redundant $C_{1,i}$ saves $33$~B).
  1064	
  1065	The compact sigma optimisation alone recovers $427$--$460$~bytes---more
  1066	than $60\%$ of the total potential saving including BP++.
  1067	It requires no change to the encryption scheme, no new cryptographic
  1068	primitives, and no specification of new ciphertext formats;
  1069	only the proof serialization changes.

   840	from the proof alone; the resulting proofs would simply be valid proofs
   841	of distinct statements that happen to share the underlying nonces.
   842	An adversary observing both proofs can extract the witnesses.
   843	
   844	\begin{remark}[Synthetic nonces]
   845	  \label{rem:synthetic-nonces}
   846	  We recommend \emph{deterministic nonce generation} following
   847	  RFC~6979~\cite{RFC6979}:
   848	  \[
   849	    (\alpha_m, \ldots, \alpha_{\sk}) \;\leftarrow\;
   850	    \mathsf{HKDF}\bigl(
   851	    \sk_A \| r \| m \| b \| \rho \| \text{statement}
   852	    \| \text{fresh entropy}
   853	    \bigr).
   854	  \]
   855	  Including the full witness ensures nonces are unique per transaction.
   856	  The optional fresh entropy provides defense-in-depth.
   857	\end{remark}
   858	
   859	\subsection{Fiat--Shamir Domain Separation}
   860	\label{sec:domain-sep}
   861	
   862	The hash input for the challenge (\cref{eq:fs-hash}) must include:
   863	\begin{enumerate}
   864	  \item A fixed domain-separation tag
   865	        (\texttt{"CMPT\_SEND\_SIGMA"}).
   866	  \item \emph{All} public-key and ciphertext components:
   867	        the recipient keys $P_1, \ldots, P_n$, the sender key~$P_A$,
   868	        the ciphertext bundle $C_1, C_{2,1}, \ldots, C_{2,n}$, the
   869	        commitments $\mathit{PC}_m, \mathit{PC}_b$, and the balance
   870	        ciphertext~$(B_1, B_2)$.
   871	  \item All first-round commitments
   872	        $T_1, T_{2,1}, \ldots, T_{2,n}, T_m, T_b, T_{\sk,1}, T_{\sk,2}$.
   873	  \item The \TransCtx{} for replay protection.
   874	\end{enumerate}
   875	Omitting any statement element from the hash would allow
   876	cross-protocol attacks.
   877	The domain-separation tag prevents collisions with other
   878	Fiat--Shamir proofs in the CMPT protocol.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	TITLE: [Docs] Paper §2.1 says C₁ is sent once, but each Send field carries its own copy, so the size tables are 99 bytes low
     2	REPO: XRPLF/mpt-crypto
     3	
     4	---
     5	
     6	`paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex` §2.1 says *"The shared first component C₁ is transmitted once on-chain"*, and the paper totals a Send at 1177 bytes for n = 4. That does not match the wire format. rippled requires each encrypted-amount field to be a full 66-byte ciphertext, so `C₁` is sent once per participant. The real sizes are 1276 bytes for n = 4 and 1210 for n = 3, and I measured both on Devnet.
     7	
     8	**Suggested fix:** correct §2.1 and the totals to the wire format, or say that the shared-C₁ layout is a proposal rather than what rippled uses.
     9	
    10	**The component sums**
    11	
    12	```text
    13	Component           Paper (Table, lines 1002-1017)     Wire (rippled)
    14	C1                  1 x 33       = 33                  inside each field below
    15	Ciphertexts         n x 33 (C2)  = 132 / 99            n x 66 (C1 || C2) = 264 / 198
    16	Amount commitment   33                                 33
    17	Balance commitment  33                                 33
    18	Proofs              192 + 754    = 946                 946
    19	Total, n = 4 / 3    1177 / 1144                        1276 / 1210
    20	Difference                                             (n - 1) x 33 = 99 / 66
    21	```
    22	
    23	The extra copies carry no information. The library refuses a Send unless every participant's first 33 bytes equal the first one's: *"All participants must share the same c1 bytes"* ([`mpt_utility.cpp` lines 1140-1145](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/blob/3baaaac33dadec3e649846e487f452c5b6b2d825/src/utility/mpt_utility.cpp#L1140-L1145)).
    24

    28	- The 1177 total: the abstract, [lines 98-100](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/blob/3baaaac33dadec3e649846e487f452c5b6b2d825/paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex#L98-L100); the introduction, [176-179](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/blob/3baaaac33dadec3e649846e487f452c5b6b2d825/paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex#L176-L179); and three tables, [1017](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/blob/3baaaac33dadec3e649846e487f452c5b6b2d825/paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex#L1017), [1049](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/blob/3baaaac33dadec3e649846e487f452c5b6b2d825/paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex#L1049) and [1633](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/blob/3baaaac33dadec3e649846e487f452c5b6b2d825/paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex#L1633).
    29	- The wire rule: rippled's Send preflight checks each field against a 66-byte length ([`ConfidentialMPTSend.cpp` lines 51-61](https://github.com/XRPLF/rippled/blob/6641896e9fd823cf3665c7a36bb3da47646e3b27/src/libxrpl/tx/transactors/token/ConfidentialMPTSend.cpp#L51-L61)), with `kMPT_ELGAMAL_TOTAL_SIZE 66` ([`mpt_protocol.h` line 29](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/blob/3baaaac33dadec3e649846e487f452c5b6b2d825/include/mpt_protocol.h#L29)).

Each encrypted-amount field was 66 bytes, and the fields summed to 1210 bytes without an auditor and 1276 with one. The field checks above are unchanged at rippled `develop` today. I have not re-measured.

/bin/zsh -lc "python3 - <<'PY'
import re
body=r'''"'`paper/cmpt-compact-sigma.tex` §2.1 says *"The shared first component C₁ is transmitted once on-chain"* and reports 1177 bytes for n = 4 and 1144 for n = 3. That is not the current wire format. `ConfidentialMPTSend` requires each of the three mandatory encrypted-amount fields, plus the optional auditor field, to be a 66-byte `C₁ || C₂` ciphertext. `mpt_protocol.h` defines that total as 66 bytes. At n = 3 the participants are sender, destination and issuer; n = 4 adds the auditor.

```text
                         n = 4   n = 3
Paper: 1×33 + n×33 + 2×33 + 946  1177    1144
Wire:  n×66 + 2×33 + 946            1276    1210
Difference: (n−1)×33                  99      66
```

These are cryptographic-payload totals only: encrypted amounts, two commitments and the 946-byte proof. They are not the complete signed transaction or its field framing. Current XLS-0096 §17.1 already gives 1276 and 1210 and explicitly excludes ledger metadata and transaction headers.

The verifier also requires every participant'"'s first 33 bytes to match the first participant's. Rippled's "'`ConfidentialTransfer.cpp` calls this verifier during `ConfidentialMPTSend::preclaim`, and current `develop` still requires `mpt-crypto/1.0.2`. The repeated `C₁` bytes are therefore redundant, but they are still serialized in each field.

Current `main` still contains the paper'"'s sentence and totals, while current "'`develop` still uses the 66-byte fields. Please update the paper to match the implementation and XLS-0096, or explicitly label the shared-`C₁` layout as a proposal rather than deployed wire format.'"'''
print(len(re.findall(r\"\\b[\\w’'-]+\\b\", re.sub(r'"'```.*?```'"','',body,flags=re.S))))
PY" in $USER_TMP/T/f13r1.v6b2o3

    52	**draft text and the frozen primary sources**, and every finding is resolved.
    53	
    54	⚠ **A review that runs after submission cannot prevent anything.** It can only
    55	produce a correction, and a correction is a public record that the filing was
    56	not ready.
    57	
    58	**The reviewer is given the draft and the sources, not the conclusion.**
    59	
    60	### 2. A filing is at most 300 words, with one concrete ask
    61	
    62	Counted over the issue body, excluding code blocks, quoted source and the
    63	disclosure footer.
    64	
    65	⚠ **`WORD_LIMIT` in `tools/check-filing.ts` is what enforces this, and the gate
    66	prints the number it used.** This heading states the rule; that constant is the
    67	instrument. If the two ever disagree, the gate is right and this line is the
    68	defect — and neither the skills nor a draft may restate the number at all.
    69	
    70	- **One ask per issue.** If there are five asks, that is five issues or one
    71	  errata list with five clearly numbered items and no argument between them.
    72	- **Evidence is linked, not inlined.** A permalink to a line range beats a
    73	  quotation; a quotation beats a paragraph explaining the quotation.
    74	- **State the defect, the evidence, and the fix.** Nothing else.
    75	
    76	⚠ The 2026-08-26 filings averaged **~750 words**. The dev-portal one was a
    77	two-sentence documentation fix in six sections. A maintainer who cannot find the
    78	ask in fifteen seconds will not look for it.
    79	
    80	### 3. No "positives" filings to a bug tracker
    81	
    82	An issue tracker is for things that need doing. Appreciation is not.
    83	
    84	⚠ **And on `rippled` it is not even accepted**: `blank_issues_enabled: false`,
    85	with only a bug report and a feature request offered.
    86	
    87	⚠ `rippled#8118` was *"four design decisions that were better than they
    88	looked"*. It needed a same-day correction because its central claim was false —
    89	the spec advertises the property **twice**, in wording close to what the filing
    90	presented as its own reading — and a core contributor **closed it without
    91	comment**.
    92	
   122	  and bold blocks are internal instruments; in a filing they read as shouting.
   123	
   124	---
   125	
   126	## Before sending — the checklist
   127	
   128	1. `make check-filing FILE=<draft>` passes.
   129	2. An adversarial review has run on the draft and every finding is resolved.
   130	3. Every citation re-fetched at source **today**, with the ref recorded.
   131	4. Every negative claim carries its search.
   132	5. The Trail of Bits April 2026 review has been checked — ⚠ several things this
   133	   project first recorded as findings were already found and fixed there.
   134	6. The ask is one sentence and appears in the first paragraph.
   135	7. Authorisation from the project owner **for this filing**. ⚠ Authorisation is
   136	   never standing; it is per filing, per session.
   137	
   138	---
   139	
   140	## After sending
   141	
   142	- Archive the exact submitted text under `docs/upstream-filings/<date>/`. A
   143	  GitHub issue can be edited; the archive is what the register points at.
   144	- Record the issue URL in `UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md` and move the row to `Filed`.
   145	- ⚠ **If a correction becomes necessary, post it as a comment and leave the
    65	**So attack the claims before you touch the prose.**
    66	
    67	### Verify every claim against the primary source, now
    68	
    69	- Open the source the draft cites. **Do not trust the draft's quotation** — a
    70	  filed row once quoted a sentence the specification has never contained
    71	  (`UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md` U-15).
    72	- Check line numbers and section numbers still resolve. Sources move.
    73	- ⚠ **Every negative claim needs its search, and you run it.** *"There is no
    74	  such field"*, *"not called out anywhere"*, *"not exported"* — one search
    75	  settles it. **`rippled#8118` said a property was not advertised anywhere; the
    76	  specification states it twice**, once in wording close to what the draft
    77	  presented as its own reading.
    78	- Check whether upstream already fixed it. **Four rows died as stale gaps** —
    79	  `UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md` U-3, U-8, U-9, U-10 — each describing something already closed.
    80	- Check the Trail of Bits April 2026 review. Several things this project first
    81	  recorded as findings were already found and fixed there. Read Appendices B to D
    82	  too: the fix review does not cover them, and F-19 was in Appendix B
    83	  (`CLAIMS.md` E-296).
    84	
    85	### Separate the three layers
    86	
    87	**Demonstrated** — you ran it or read it at source.
    88	**Inferred** — it follows, but nobody checked.
    89	**Proposed** — what the draft wants done.
    90	
    91	⚠ A draft that presents the second or third as the first is the defect that
    92	needed four public corrections. Say which layer each claim is on.
    93	
    94	⚠ **Do not let the draft's framing set yours.** Ask what would have to be true
    95	for the claim to be *wrong*, and go and check that.
    96	
    97	## Then the maintainer's read
    98	
    99	**Actionable?** Can a maintainer tell what to investigate, decide or change?
   100	
   101	**Scoped?** One independently actionable problem, or several disguised as one?
   102	
   103	**Skimmable?** Headings, bullets, code blocks, tables. Conclusion before
   104	evidence.
   105	
   106	**Something to do?** ⚠ A positives-only filing does not belong on a bug tracker
   107	at all — reject it rather than editing it.

codex
The component arithmetic and core wire-format claim survive verification, and the historical Devnet measurement is honestly dated and disclosed. However, the draft is over the word limit, contains a misleading Trail of Bits revision claim, lacks current verification-path references, and omits the current XLS-0096 confirmation; it is not ready to file. The Zellic PR found by the owner-wide search concerns unrelated API hardening and need not be named in the issue.

Full review comments:

- [P1] Correct the Trail of Bits audit note before filing — $USER_TMP/T/f13r1.v6b2o3/draft.md:50-53
  The linked PDF is a “Comprehensive Report with Fix Review,” and pp. 66–67 mark TOB-RIPCTX-16 resolved. That finding is directly related: it discusses the specification’s shared C1 requirement, although its focus is proof serialization rather than ciphertext-field serialization. Saying it is unrelated and that no later revision was obtained is misleading; replace it with the precise distinction and current fix status. This follows `REVIEW-INSTRUCTIONS.md:78-83`.

- [P2] Scope the totals to the cryptographic payload — $USER_TMP/T/f13r1.v6b2o3/draft.md:6-6
  The arithmetic is correct for encrypted amounts, two commitments, and the 946-byte proof, but calling 1276/1210 the “real sizes” and “wire format” can imply complete serialized transaction sizes. Account, destination, issuance, signatures, field framing, and other transaction metadata are excluded. Current XLS-0096 §17.1 explicitly makes this exclusion. State that these are cryptographic-payload totals to avoid overstating the defect.

- [P2] Add current refs and the rippled verification path — $USER_TMP/T/f13r1.v6b2o3/draft.md:23-23
  The shared-C1 check is on rippled’s Send path: current `develop` calls `verifySendProofs`, which calls `mpt_verify_send_proof`, and `conanfile.py` still requires `mpt-crypto/1.0.2`. The draft only links an older preflight commit and does not record current-HEAD permalinks or hashes, so “unchanged ... today” is not reproducible from the filing. Add the current `develop` path and refs, as required by `PROTOCOL.md:126-133`.

- [P1] Cut the body below the filing word limit — $USER_TMP/T/f13r1.v6b2o3/draft.md:31-33
  Counting the issue body while excluding the fenced table, `<details>` block, block quotes, and disclosure footer gives 334 words, so the draft exceeds the filing gate. Remove the repeated source list and make the historical measurement a brief non-load-bearing note or omit it; the code and specification determine the totals. `PROTOCOL.md:60-74` requires the word limit and one concrete ask.

- [P2] Cite XLS-0096 and narrow the documentation ask — $USER_TMP/T/f13r1.v6b2o3/draft.md:8-8
  The repository and XLS-0096 searches found no indication that shared-C1 serialization is a planned wire change, while current XLS-0096 §17.1 already documents 66-byte ciphertexts and the corrected 1276/1210 payload totals. The actionable defect is therefore paper/spec consistency, not a missing rippled implementation. Cite that section and state that line 8 is one documentation ask with two acceptable resolutions, consistent with `PROTOCOL.md:70-74`.
